# Pathway Forward

Record of tasks done and open issues for this repo. See
`PROJECT_RULES.md` for the rules each item enforces. History (what shipped,
when) lives in `pastReleaseNotes.md` and `README.md` — this file does not
duplicate it and is not append-only; items are updated or closed in place.

A blank "Last checked" means never audited and stays blank until someone
actually runs the command.

**Struck-through (resolved) rows record the state AT THE TIME they were
closed**, so they name files that have since been deleted or moved -- notably
`check.test.py`, `testAll.py`, `src/pydump.f90`, the `accept` and `parity`
tiers, and the pre-restructure Python module names (`port_rsf.py`,
`kernels_numpy.py`, `standalone/main.py`). That is history, not drift, and it
is not rewritten. The `Command` column of an OPEN row, by contrast, must run
today -- if one does not, that is a bug in this file.

Current layout, for reading the older rows: Fortran is `src/fortran/`, the
Python solver is `src/python/eqdyna/` (15 modules against Fortran's 26 `.f90`
files, since `MPI4NodalQuant.py` joined 2026-09-21 -- each module is named
after its Fortran counterpart, except `backend.py` and `__main__.py`, which
have none by design, `assembleGlobalKU.py`, which covers four Fortran files
fused into one loop, and `MPI4NodalQuant.py`, whose counterpart is embedded
inside `assembleGlobalMass.f90:58-245` rather than its own file; see
`CLAUDE.md:25-29`), and
there is ONE test -- `testsys/run.py e2e`, 10 cases x 3 backends = 30 cells,
plus a fourth backend axis, `python-jax-mpi`, opt-in per case (one case
opted in as of 2026-09-21 -- see item 43); the table now declares 40 cells,
31 of them runnable. See `CLAUDE.md`'s "There is ONE test" for the full
account.

**Perf-ledger wall-clock rows are only comparable within their tenancy.**
`docs/perf_ledger.jsonl` gained 66 rows on 2026-09-22 from two full
`testsys/run.py all` sweeps plus the GPU runs: the same 31 cells took 9413.8s
on a box shared with other agents' jobs (load average ~100 on 64 cores --
item 61's gate-d4f625e evidence) and 2904.6s / 3340.2s on quieter boxes (item
58's gate-a16cc98 evidence). Parity -- the actual pass/fail bound -- is
load-independent; wall clock is not, and rule 6's provenance fields (machine,
core count, compiler, git SHA) do not currently capture contention. Do not
compare a ledger wall-clock row against another without first checking they
ran under comparable load. A candidate rule-6 sub-rule to make this
mechanical is proposed here, not written into `PROJECT_RULES.md` by this
board pass.

## Priority order (set 2026-09-16)

Nine columns, fixed: `rank | item | what | status / why this rank | rules |
surface | blocked on / re-check | last checked | command`. A row with fewer
cells than that silently drops the ones past the header's width, which is how
an unrunnable command hid in plain sight until 2026-09-21 -- pad short rows
with empty cells rather than omitting them, and escape any literal `|` inside
a cell as `\|` (an unescaped one starts a new column mid-sentence).

Ranked by value per unit of risk, not by item number. P1 first; do not start a
P3 whose prerequisite is unmet. **BLOCKED-ON-OWNER items need a decision, not
effort** -- doing them anyway means guessing at something that is not mine to
guess.

| **P1** | 32 | MEASURED 2026-09-16, and the additive model is REFUTED. drv.a6's rupture-arrival flips are a FIXED MARGINAL POPULATION, not a sum of error sources. The experiment: compare serial python against a SERIAL FORTRAN reference, removing the decomposition term by construction. Prediction was ~90 flips (port alone) after subtracting a ~330 decomposition floor. **Removing decomposition made it WORSE.** fortran-serial vs 4-rank **372**; python-jax vs 4-rank **415**; python-numpy vs 4-rank **423**; python-jax vs fortran-serial **442**; python-numpy vs fortran-serial **450** (exactly AT the budget). Every pair lands in 372-450 of 5151 nodes (7-9%), and every pair has median \|dfnft\| = **0.0417 s = exactly one time step**. That is the signature of ~400 nodes whose arrest is decided at roundoff: ANY two runs differing at all -- rank count, language, summation order -- disagree on most of them, and the count measures the POPULATION SIZE, not the perturbation magnitude. So '329 decomposition + 90 port' was a model the data does not support, and neither does 'the port is responsible for 415'. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE RECORD: (i) this session earlier reported **329** for fortran-serial vs 4-rank; it reproduces at **372**, matching item 18/21's recorded A=372. The 329 has no recoverable provenance and is DISCARDED, not averaged. (ii) python-numpy vs a serial reference is exactly 450/450, so if drv.a6's reference were ever re-frozen from a serial run the gate would sit at its limit with zero headroom -- the 450 budget is specific to the 4-rank reference it was calibrated against. WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY SETTLE THE PHYSICS: refine the mesh. If the marginal population is a discretisation artifact its FRACTION shrinks at dx=250; if the fault is genuinely bistable at this roughness and friction, the fraction persists. That is now the open question, and it is a different experiment from the one this item used to propose. Reproduce with `testsys/parity/evidence_drv_a6_marginal_population.py`. **RESULT 2026-09-22 (recovered and measured by wei-lin, conductor #4):** the refinement experiment this row called for was NOT in flight -- both dx=250 runs (4-rank and serial) had exited 0 at 2026-09-22 00:04 and sat unanalysed for ~21h because the launching agent was rate-limited before running the comparison. Recovered by running the experiment's own `flips.py` (imports the committed `testsys.compare.flip_decomposition` + `matrix.DRV_A6` verbatim -- no new flip definition). Instrument verified on a known answer FIRST: dx=500 baseline re-derivation -- nodes 5151, ruptured-both 1075, only-A 176, only-B 153, timing-shifts 43, **TOTAL FLIPS 372, FRACTION 7.222%, median\|dfnft\| 0.0417 s, phys_max 2.122e+07** -- matches item 18/21's recorded A=372 and its 2.12e7 max diff to every printed digit. The experiment, dx=250 4-rank vs dx=250 serial: nodes 20301, ruptured-both 4554, only-A 979, only-B 966, timing-shifts 397, **TOTAL FLIPS 2342, FRACTION 11.536%, median\|dfnft\| 0.0208 s, phys_max 4.143e+07**. Against the pre-registered kill criteria (stated before any run): discretisation-artifact predicted constant COUNT ~370/fraction ~1.8% -- measured count is 2342 (x6.30 against a node count that grew x3.94); bistability predicted constant FRACTION ~7.2%/count ~1470 -- measured fraction grew x1.60. **THE DISCRETISATION-ARTIFACT HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED, and not marginally** -- the marginal population grew super-proportionally to resolution; the bistability branch had the direction right and the magnitude low. median\|dfnft\| halved to exactly one time step at half dx, so the roundoff-decided-arrest signature is unchanged in character, only its extent grew. Caveat carried, not buried: the dx=500 pair is a fresh serial run against a committed 4-rank reference frozen at an earlier commit, while the dx=250 pair is same-binary/same-session serial vs 4-rank -- that asymmetry can only INFLATE the dx=500 number, so it cannot manufacture the observed growth. **NOT CLOSED HERE.** `test.drv.a6`'s reference and flip budget were not touched and must not be (rule 7; hands-off this campaign). OWNER's question, unanswered: does this settle the item-32 numerics question, and should a fault measurably bistable at this roughness continue to be gated by a flip BUDGET at all. Root cause NOT fully pinned: a synthetic microbenchmark of the einsum-signature change in `compute_element_shape` (`'ik,ekj->eji'` -> `'eik,ekj->eji'`, needed for the per-element wedge/non-wedge derivative select) came back bit-identical for non-wedge elements in isolation, so the actual divergence is somewhere else in the same 3 files -- plausibly a JAX-specific effect (XLA may fuse/schedule the whole traced graph differently once elemType-conditional branches exist in it, even on a mesh where every branch evaluates false, unlike NumPy's eager per-op execution -- consistent with python-numpy NOT failing while python-jax did). Sent back to mira-volkov with this exact isolation recipe rather than the port itself, since finding the precise mechanism is a real debugging task, not a re-review. Do not re-attempt the merge without a fresh full 9-case/27-cell sweep confirming tpv8 x jax (and every other non-wedge cell) unchanged. Also blocked on reference-freeze-creates-new-ground-truth (rule 7). **RESOLVED 2026-09-16 (fresh worktree, re-derived from master `ad48856`, since the debugging worktree above was already reaped): root cause PINNED, not the jax-scheduling hypothesis.** `compute_element_shape`'s `eleshp` contraction used ONE per-element einsum (`'erc,eck->erk'`) for every element regardless of wedge/non-wedge, replacing the pre-wedge-port invariant-operand einsum (`'erc,ck->erk'`). On test.tpv8's real mesh (235,008 elements, zero wedge) this is a genuine NumPy bug, not a jax artifact: measured directly by loading the OLD and NEW `compute_element_shape` as standalone modules against the SAME real tpv8 `xl`/`elem_type` arrays, `eleshp` differs (max\|diff\|=2.168e-19, 18,462/235,008 elements) purely from NumPy's einsum optimizer dispatching the invariant-operand form to a BLAS batched matmul (FMA) and the per-element form to a generic no-FMA loop. `compute_element_shape` runs ONCE, host-side, in plain NumPy for EITHER backend (`eqdyna3d.py`'s `build_solver_state`, never under `jax.jit`) -- confirmed by reading the call site, not assumed -- so the divergence and its amplification-difference are backend-agnostic; only the 114-step nonlinear rupture dynamics happened to amplify the same tiny perturbation past jax's bound (1e-8) while numpy stayed under by chance. The earlier "XLA schedules a per-element-conditional graph differently" hypothesis does NOT hold and is retracted. FIX: `compute_element_shape` now dispatches the `eleshp` contraction BY MASK -- non-wedge elements get the exact pre-wedge-port einsum back (confirmed bit-identical to the old code on tpv8's real mesh), wedge elements keep the per-element form. | **P2** | 60 | ~~jax-MPI 32-rank plateau (5.28x vs Fortran's 37.33x) HAS A MECHANISM ... Gated at bit-identity (rule 5a), reproduced independently by the board editor. The DRAM-vs-LLC split (item 63) is UNCHANGED by this fix and stays open | REWRITTEN A SECOND TIME 2026-09-22: the plateau now HAS a mechanism, and two exclusions this row asserted as fact are dead. **Mechanism (relayed from `docs/NOTES_plateau_32rank_2026-09-22.md` -- 374 lines, `mira-volkov`'s own measurement record, merged at `f278056`; the board editor re-ran neither the probes nor the sweep and says so):** the carry is allocated at the GLOBAL problem size on EVERY rank -- `src/python/eqdyna/driver.py:382-389`, `v1 + velArr + dispArr + force` = 97.75 MB, byte-identical at 1 rank and at 32, and 98.5% of the entire 32-rank carry; `mass` is global too (`driver.py:367`); `src/python/eqdyna/MPI4NodalQuant.py:183-190` restricts the index arrays but NOT the arrays they address, and `:36-52` records that as a MEMORY cost -- measured, it is a BANDWIDTH cost. Consequence: per-rank per-step traffic floors at ~100 MB in and ~100 MB out however many ranks there are, so from 4 ranks up the step is memory-system-bound on replicated global nodal state rather than compute-bound on the element work that was actually divided, and the rank-independent residual `C` (103-126 ms, no trend in N) IS that traffic plus the contention it causes. **Evidence split by rule 4a into defect side and outcome side, because that distinction is what this row got wrong last time:** DEFECT side = the 97.75 MB of replication, plus `div` (`driver.py:177`) reading 7.40 ms at 1 rank and 7.23-7.54 ms at 32 -- flat in rank count, 1.94 ns per global equation on tpv104 against 1.86 ns on tpv8, pre-registered as a falsifiable prediction BEFORE measuring and held. OUTCOME side, which is what makes this a cause and not just a defect = the same rank-local work costs 51.62 ms ALONE and 97.94 ms in situ at 32 ranks (1.90x), and **32 concurrent INDEPENDENT, ZERO-COMMUNICATION processes reproduce it at 94.30 ms, within 3.7% of in situ** -- with MPI, the barrier and the exchange removed entirely, the cost still appears. 32-rank attribution on the rank that SETS the step (`EQDYNA_MPI_STEP_PROFILE=1`): compute 97.94 \| barrier 2.56 \| mpi 5.78 \| halo d2h 0.22 \| host 18.11 \| TOTAL 124.60 ms, i.e. 78.6% jitted compute. Donation is EFFECTIVE (`is_deleted()` True on all four global arrays; `b_jit` alias=arg=out=99.21 MB, temp 0.25 MB), so the ~120 MB/step carry copy `driver.py:410-417` warns about is NOT being paid and was never the story either. **The two dead exclusions: (1) CONTENTION IS NOT EXCLUDED -- it is the largest single term, ~45 ms of a ~124 ms step.** The per-rank WALL spread of 1.00x this row cited is an IDENTITY of a barrier-synchronised loop, and `EFFECTIVE_CORES` = 1.00 is blind to memory stalls; the compute spread underneath is 2.90x (33.73-97.94 ms) against a work spread of 1.41x. That pair of blind metrics is now `PROJECT_RULES.md` rule 4b, written from this row. **(2) element BALANCE stays disproved** (recut took zero-`Ei` ranks 4 -> 0 and predicted work spread 3.350x -> 1.004x; the 32-rank per-step cost moved 1.4%, inside this box's run-to-run variation). Halo/exchange stays excluded and is now excluded by an instrument that can see it: mpi 1.01-9.16 ms, halo d2h 0.21-0.32 ms of a 124.6 ms step. **NOT CLOSED, stated as the measurer stated it:** DRAM bandwidth vs shared-LLC capacity is NOT separated -- a FIXED workload degrades only 1.37x from concurrency 1 -> 32 (50.09 -> 68.80 ms, flat for K=2..16), so part of the worst rank's 1.90x is POSITION-dependent (cpu/NUMA placement); splitting it needs `perf` counters, which were not read and are explicitly not claimed. **Two caveats the board editor adds from reading the artifact rather than the summary:** (a) the no-perturbation figure is established at 8 ranks ONLY (212.43 profiled vs 211.29 committed unprofiled, 0.5%) -- the profiled 32-rank step reads 123-125 ms against the committed unprofiled 143.27 ms, attributed to box load (17.8 vs 10.6) with no control run; (b) the uncontended max of 51.62 ms is rank 16 of 32, whose `div` reads 15.65 ms in the same table where every other rank reads 7.23-7.54 -- so either that row is contaminated (making the inflation 97.94/50.11 = 1.95x) or `div` is not flat after all, and the artifact does not say which. Neither caveat touches the concurrency probe, which is the decisive measurement. The imbalance was REAL and is now GONE and the curve did not move. Defect side: `Ei` (elements owned) was ZERO on four of the 32 ranks against ~19,000 on the rest, barrier wait 105 ms of a 172.6 ms step (61%), corroborated the same night on 4xA100 (item 59's row: 48% spread, 104112/104832/153216/153936, barrier 1.78-3.70 ms of a 9.54 ms step). Cause of the defect: `PML_WEIGHT = 3.0` at `src/python/eqdyna/MPI4NodalQuant.py:85`, which the source itself documents as a GUESS, is FIVE TIMES too high -- the measured PML/interior cost ratio is ~0.6. Recutting at w=0.56 on the real `test.tpv104` mesh took zero-`Ei` ranks 4 -> 0, predicted work spread 3.350x -> 1.004x, and `straggler` to 1.00 at EVERY rank count. Outcome side, per-step cost w=3.0 -> w=0.6: 8 ranks 172.63 ms (4.35x) -> 202.90 ms (3.73x); 16 ranks 171.64 ms (4.37x) -> 180.80 ms (4.18x); 32 ranks 134.12 ms (5.59x) -> 132.20 ms (5.72x). The 1.4% at 32 ranks is inside this box's run-to-run variation; the 8-rank point moved 17.5% and the 16-rank point 5.3% the WRONG way, each a SINGLE measurement on a shared box, so both are UNRESOLVED and NEITHER is reported as a regression. So the barrier wait was a SYMPTOM, and of what is now specific: a MEMORY-induced compute spread (2.90x), not an element-count spread (1.41x) -- which is exactly why the recut bought 1.4%. Why Fortran does not have this: `src/fortran/meshgen.f90` gives each rank its OWN rank-locally-numbered mesh, so its nodal arrays SHRINK with rank count, while the port keeps the proven serial mesh on every rank and restricts only the indices -- which is why the port's indices cannot be wrong and why its bytes cannot shrink. **THE FIX IS NOT QUEUED AND IS NOT A MISSION: shrinking the carry to rank-local extent changes the memory layout of a GATED path and is product behaviour, so it is the owner's ruling -- recorded here alongside item 61's, not effort anyone should start.** The structural finding that survives is item 62 (`Ei > 0` is a canary, not a goal), now STRENGTHENED rather than contradicted. `b6106af` (`PML_WEIGHT` 3.0 -> 0.6) is correct by measurement with NO demonstrated benefit and is NOT landing; it is parity-clean (**CORRECTED 2026-09-22, board editor:** the figure this row and item 61's carried, `test.tpv8` x `python-jax-mpi` SUCCESS max\|diff\| 3.05e-11, is misattributed -- 3.051760e-11 is `test.tpv8` x `fortran`'s gate value; the jax-mpi cell's own printed value, both before and after `01a1040`, is **1.220703e-10** vs bound 1.0e-08; `test.tpv8` x `python-jax` SUCCESS 4.12e-10) and is preserved with item 61's `14f0b7f` on `origin/mira/mpi-balance-and-cache-2026-09-22` (`2ce6c0d`). **RESOLVED 2026-09-22, owner ruled, LANDED at `01a1040`** ("perf: give every jax-MPI rank a real subdomain -- rank-local carry, bit-identical", mira-volkov, 12 files, +3250/-98). The fix this row's "BLOCKED ON OWNER" was waiting on: `MPI4NodalQuant` now renumbers every index array into the rank-local `touched`/`my_eq` sets the restriction already computed (see `_relabel`'s docstring, `NOTES_item43_mpi.md`), and the carry (`v1`/`velArr`/`dispArr`/`force`) plus the lumped mass are sized on `inv_l['N']`/`inv_l['NEQ']` instead of the global extent. MEASURED (mira's own table, Fortran measured back-to-back in the SAME session -- relayed, not independently re-run by the board editor): carry/rank down 27.3x over 32 ranks (181.52 -> 6.64 MB); jax ms/step by rank count, before -> after (fortran same-session alongside): 1 rank 756.92 -> 755.61 (fortran 921.26); 4 ranks 315.31 -> 284.36 (230.29); 16 ranks 150.04 -> 93.34 (61.93); 32 ranks 143.27 -> 45.76 (33.91). jax's own 1-to-32-rank speedup: 5.28x -> 16.51x. jax/fortran ratio at 32 ranks, **computed like-for-like against this session's own 33.91 ms/step Fortran denominator** (not the 24.71 ms/step figure item 48 used, which came from a different, less-contended session): before 143.27/33.91 = **4.22x** slower, after 45.76/33.91 = **1.35x** slower -- the "5.80x -> 1.35x" figure elsewhere on this board mixes denominators (5.80x = 143.27/24.71, the stale one) and should not be read as a like-for-like pair; see item 48's row, corrected in step. **INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED 2026-09-22 (board editor, fresh, not relayed):** ran the gated cell on the branch, then re-ran the identical `mpirun -np 4 python3 -u -m eqdyna <case_dir> --backend jax --mpi` (`EQDYNA_MPI_SYNC=halo`, `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`) against MASTER's (pre-`01a1040`) solver tree on the same case dir and placement, and compared raw rank files byte-for-byte: `frt.txt0` 52536 bytes/132 rows, `frt.txt1` 329942/829, `frt.txt2` 320788/806, `frt.txt3` 49352/124 -- IDENTICAL and md5-equal on all four pairs, row counts matching `matrix.PY_MPI_EXPECTED_FRT_FILES` exactly. The mechanism is visible rather than asserted: the four ranks reported FOUR DIFFERENT carry sizes (16.14/17.57/17.53/16.18 MB; `NEQ_local` 365424/367878/367872/365427) where at global extent all four were byte-identical -- **gated at bit-identity, not at the case bound (new sub-rule 5a, written from this landing)**. | **P2** | 63 | DRAM bandwidth vs shared-LLC capacity (item 60's open edge) CANNOT BE MEASURED ON THIS BOX -- the blocker is PRIVILEGE, not physics | NEW 2026-09-22 (conductor #4, worktree hygiene pass). Four probes, all run: `perf_event_paranoid` = 4 denies unprivileged core-PMU access outright (`perf stat -e LLC-load-misses,LLC-loads,cache-references` refuses without CAP_PERFMON/CAP_SYS_PTRACE/CAP_SYS_ADMIN; `sudo -n true` demands a password). More decisive: `/sys/bus/event_source/devices/` enumerates only `breakpoint cpu ibs_fetch ibs_op kprobe msr power software tracepoint uprobe` -- NO `amd_df`/`amd_umc`/`uncore_imc` node. On AMD Rome the DRAM counters live in the Data Fabric PMU, which this box does not expose at all, so the perf route would not produce a DRAM number even at `paranoid=0`. `/dev/cpu/*/msr` is root-only, closing the manual route too. **Rule 4b applied to this exclusion itself: the metric that produced it is INSTRUMENT AVAILABILITY (`perf_event_open` EACCES plus a sysfs enumeration), not a measurement of either mechanism -- so nothing here excludes DRAM bandwidth and nothing here excludes shared-LLC capacity.** Both remain live; `mira-volkov`'s refusal to claim the split (item 60's row) stands as the correct call. **The one root action that WOULD answer it, and it is better than the LLC-miss-rate proxy originally specified:** this CPU advertises `cqm_llc`/`cqm_occup_llc`/`cqm_mbm_total`/`cqm_mbm_local` (AMD QoS monitoring) and the kernel supports the filesystem that exposes them (`nodev resctrl` in `/proc/filesystems`), but `/sys/fs/resctrl` is unmounted and `mount -t resctrl resctrl /sys/fs/resctrl` needs root ("must be superuser to use mount"). Mounted, it gives per-process-group `llc_occupancy` (shared-LLC capacity, bytes) and `mbm_local_bytes`/`mbm_total_bytes` (DRAM traffic, bytes) DIRECTLY, per rank, at 1/4/8/16/32 -- the ONLY route on this box, since `amd_df` is absent. Blocked on one command: `sudo mount -t resctrl resctrl /sys/fs/resctrl`. Cache geometry, recorded as ARITHMETIC and explicitly NOT as an exclusion: 2x AMD EPYC 7532, 64 cores, 8 NUMA nodes, L3 512 MiB in 32 instances = 16 MiB per instance, 2 cores per instance (Rome L3 is per-CCX, not box-wide) -- the 97.75 MB per-rank carry (item 60) is ~6.1x a whole L3 instance at ANY rank count including 1, and the per-rank slice only falls 16 -> ~8 MiB from 1 to 32 ranks (a 2x cut in a resource already 6x too small), while the Data Fabric and memory controllers are shared 32-way and the L3 only 2-way. Read together those numbers *suggest* the 32-way-shared path is the likelier seat of the measured 1.90x inflation than the 2-way-shared one -- that is inference from sizes, not a measurement, and under rule 4b it excludes nothing. It constrains the next experiment's design; it closes no door. | 4b | `/sys/bus/event_source/devices/`, `/sys/fs/resctrl`, `/proc/filesystems`, item 60's row (the mechanism this would help split) | OWNER: one root command, `sudo mount -t resctrl resctrl /sys/fs/resctrl` (weaker alternative, still incomplete: `sudo sysctl -w kernel.perf_event_paranoid=0`, core PMU only, still no DRAM counter on this box) | 2026-09-22 | `cat /proc/sys/kernel/perf_event_paranoid; ls /sys/bus/event_source/devices/; grep resctrl /proc/filesystems; ls /sys/fs/resctrl/` (all four reproduce the blocker without root) |

Partition count vs pinned cores (four separate cold compiles, same module): 1c=none/0 annotated; 4c=2/141 annotated, 0/38 scatters; 8c=3/141, 0/38; 16c=4/141, 0/38 -- partition count is **round(sqrt(N))**, not N, and every annotated op in a dump carries the identical value. | **P3** | 47 | MEASURED 2026-09-21 (wei-lin, jax-scaling campaign, `docs/SESSION_LOG_2026-09-21_jax-scaling.md`): **box-level memory-bandwidth straggler spread on pinned MPI work** -- per-rank throughput on IDENTICAL, correctly-pinned MPI work spreads 2.68x, and it is memory bandwidth, not CPU starvation. 16 MPI ranks, each `cpus_allowed=1`, each pinned to a measured-least-loaded cpu (`mpirun --cpu-set <16 cpus> --bind-to cpu-list:ordered`), each running the same 3,818,584-element f64 jax kernel: per-rank cost spread **7.94 to 21.25 ms/step**, mean 12.62, **max/mean = 1.68x**; every rank read `EFFECTIVE_CORES` (cpu_used/wall) **0.96-1.00** -- nobody was short of CPU time, so the differentiator is shared memory bandwidth, which cannot be pinned. NUMA topology is NOT the discriminator: cpu 44 and cpu 46, both on NUMA node 5, read 7.94 vs 21.22 ms/step. Consequence: in a halo-synchronised job the slowest rank sets the step, so a 1.68x straggler spread turns an ideal 16x into ~9.5x before any collective is charged. **SCOPE LIMIT, load-bearing:** the probe is `(a*1.0000001+0.5).sum()` over a 30.5 MB array, a pure streaming kernel, maximally bandwidth-bound -- 1.68x is an UPPER BOUND on straggler severity, NOT the real solver's figure. Measured the SAME day on the SAME box, the actual FEM solver (Fortran, `test.tpv104`, 16 ranks) loses only ~1% -- 14.23x today vs the 14.39x baseline (item 43's row) -- because element-local FEM work has cache reuse a streaming sum does not. Read both numbers together, or this gets misread later as "16-rank work is capped at 9.5x here," which is false. **Practical rule, also recorded:** a pre-run busy check does NOT predict a rank's throughput -- cpus 46 and 47 both passed the under-0.45 pre-check and still produced the 21.2 ms/step stragglers -- so per-rank spread must be printed FROM the run; no pre-check substitutes for it. **Filed as its own item, not folded into 33 or 42:** it is a property of the MACHINE that outlives any one campaign, distinct from item 33 (a jax-specific scaling ask) and item 42 (full-sweep timeout reliability under self-inflicted CPU stacking) -- but it is the mechanism behind both: it explains item 33's history of moving busy-sets and SKIPs (a cpu can be idle by the busy-check and still be a bandwidth straggler over the run) and is a second, independent contention channel from item 42's CPU-oversubscription story. Cross-referenced from item 33's and item 43's rows. **CORRECTED 2026-09-22 (conductor's overnight campaign, relayed -- not independently re-run by the board editor, same disclosure as item 40/43(c)'s relayed figures):** the Sunday-recorded 2.68x per-rank spread (the min/max ratio quoted in this row's own opening sentence; the max/mean figure above is the separate 1.68x) was BOX TENANCY -- a foreign process competing for the same memory bandwidth -- not a property of the solver or its kernel. This narrows, not reverses, the mechanism above: shared memory bandwidth is still the channel, but the demand on it that produced 2.68x specifically was another tenant's, not the 16 ranks' own inherent access pattern. | **P3** | 62 | `Ei > 0` on every rank is a CANARY for "`PML_WEIGHT` exceeds the true cost ratio", NOT a decomposition goal | NEW 2026-09-22, split out of item 60 so that row carries the open plateau and this one carries the durable arithmetic underneath it. Relayed from `docs/SESSION_LOG_2026-09-22_gpu-and-32rank.md` lines 576-634, where the conductor re-derived it independently of `mira-volkov`; NOT re-derived by the board editor. On the real `test.tpv104` mesh, elements 0..31955 are ALL PML (the leading x-face slab), so under a CONTIGUOUS weighted cut rank 0 owns an interior element at 32 ranks only if `32*31956*w < Ei + Ep*w` -- with `Ei`=516096 and `Ep`=218904 that is `803688*w < 516096`, i.e. `w < 0.642`. Consequence: `Ei > 0` everywhere and a small element spread are NOT simultaneously reachable contiguously at 32 ranks for ANY weight above 0.642, and the measured true ratio (~0.6) sits just below that threshold BY ACCIDENT, not by design. So treating `Ei > 0` as a target tunes `w` below the true cost ratio and mis-costs the cut on purpose; the only sound reading is diagnostic -- a zero-`Ei` rank says `w` is above the true ratio. **STRENGTHENED 2026-09-22 from the timing side, not the arithmetic: a zero-`Ei` rank is the FASTEST measured at 32 ranks -- 36.72 ms uncontended against 50.05-51.62 ms for the interior-owning ranks -- because it still owns ~12 200 PML elements and PML is cheaper than `PML_WEIGHT` claims. Owning no interior elements is therefore not even a handicap, which removes the last reason anyone might read `Ei > 0` as a goal** (relayed from `docs/NOTES_plateau_32rank_2026-09-22.md`, not re-derived by the board editor). ### Item 43 -- jax-MPI closure: measured table, sync modes, evidence

Moved verbatim out of item 43's cell in the priority table on 2026-09-21 --
its paragraph breaks ended the table early, the same defect item 19(b)'s
account was moved out for the same day. Text unchanged; edit it here, in
place. The row's own cells (rules, surface, blocked-on, command) stayed in
the table.


| ranks | jax halo | jax allreduce | fortran | gated-mode (halo) vs fortran |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 795.05 | 785.37 | 1034.50 | 1.30x faster (allreduce 1.32x) |
| 2 | 499.49 | 423.32 | 504.83 | 1.01x faster (allreduce 1.19x) |
| 4 | 192.60 | -- | 250.17 | 1.30x faster |
| 8 | -- | -- | -- | NOT MEASURED (box tenancy -- see item 48) |
| 16 | -- | -- | -- | NOT MEASURED (box tenancy -- see item 48) |

**Two sync modes, and they are not interchangeable.** `halo` is the GATED
mode. `allreduce` is a MEASUREMENT-only mode: MPI does not promise reduction
order, so a reduction-order-dependent result must not be what a gate
compares (rule 5). The 1.32x/1.19x/1.30x figures cited elsewhere for this
work take the BEST sync mode at each rank count, and at 2 ranks that is
`allreduce`. In the GATED mode (`halo`) the 2-rank margin is **1.01x, not
1.19x** -- stated plainly so a reader does not carry the wrong number for the
mode that actually gates. **Absolute parity is MET at every rank measured**
(jax faster than Fortran at 1, 2 and 4 ranks, gated mode) -- that is what
this item closes on; it is not a correction of the result, only a sharper
read of which column backs which claim. Scaling: Fortran 1034.50 -> 504.83 ->
250.17 ms/step = 4.14x at 4 ranks; jax on its own rank-solve metric 733 ->
192.60 = 3.8x at 4 -- the MPI route tracks Fortran's curve, where `shard_map`
flattened at 2.76x at 16 devices.

Evidence, committed: `docs/perf_snapshots/mpi_scaling_2026-09-21_tpv104_mpi_vs_fortran_samesession.log`
(+`.json`) for 1 and 2 ranks, and the 4-rank Fortran column in
`..._r4_8_16.log`; the 4-rank jax value is the rank-solve difference of
388.597 ms/step at 40 steps and 241.536 at 160 from that same log
(re-derived independently: 192.5 ms/step, matching 192.60 to rounding).
`NOTES_item43_mpi.md` lines 80-130 is the full account. The contended 8/16
attempt is kept LABELLED in `..._r8_r16_CONTENDED.log` and must not be
promoted into any table -- carried forward as item 48's own row, not folded
in here as a footnote. Three measured traps from this landing are now in
`CLAUDE.md`'s "Things that will bite you": per-step-by-difference invalid at
>=4 ranks unless differenced over each rank's own solve time; `/proc/stat`
"idle" cpus that are not actually fast (`EFFECTIVE_CORES` the only real
signal); a rank owning 0 fault nodes writes no `frt` file by design, visible
only at >=4 ranks. 

### Item 19(b) / 39 -- TPV30 promotion: the full account

Moved verbatim out of the priority table on 2026-09-21 (its paragraph breaks
ended the table early, so every row below it rendered against the wrong
header). Text unchanged; edit it here, in place, and do not append another
correction on top of it.


Rule 17 steps 1-3, 5-6 are done (compset, `swtwNucleation` branch, spec-tier recording, and the SCEC-comparison script all landed and are unaffected by this closure); steps 4 and 7 (freeze, all-three-backends-pass) remain unmet and are what stay blocked.

**Established by measurement -- nobody re-chases these:** initial stress tensor -- `case.setup`'s B11/B33/B13 form and `setPlasticStress`'s R/theta form agree to 1e-6 relative, component-wise. Spec conformance -- the spec's 93%-of-yield-at-hypocenter-depth check reproduces exactly, both code paths. Plasticity params -- cohesion, bulk friction, Tv, depth taper, deviatoric ratio all verified against the spec. `str1ToFaultAngle=40.375111` -- correct per the B-coefficient derivation. Rough geometry file -- per-node gradients bit-identical from source file to solver (copied, not recomputed). **[CORRECTED 2026-09-22: the measurement was right and the reading of it was wrong -- "copied, not recomputed" was the DEFECT, not the assurance. What was copied were derivative columns subsampled from a 25 m grid (`dydx[::step]`) and handed to a coarser mesh as if they were its own slopes; bit-identical propagation of a wrong column is exactly what you would measure. Fixed on master today, derivatives recomputed at the mesh's own spacing -- see the 2026-09-22 landing paragraph below and item 31's correction.]** Fault morph -- max |y_mesh - y_file| = 5.0e-04 m over all 3321 fault nodes, against +/-1.7km of relief. `arn`/`massSlave`/`massMaster` -- 0.000e+00 relative difference across all 1921 common fault nodes vs. tpv29. `un`/`us`/`ud` -- bit-identical between tpv29 and tpv30. FE force assembly -- CORRECT, independently rebuilt from the morphed surface's own facet geometry (4 adjacent quads, each facet's own normal and area, `S.n_f*A_f` with 1/4-per-quad weighting) and matched the code's output to <0.2% per force component, <0.03% on area. Gravity body force -- cancels out of the fault traction exactly (the split-node pair's contributions are equal and opposite). Interior equilibrium -- tpv30's interior-element force residual has mean 3e-04 m/s2, the bulk field is fine. Boundary tractions -- missing (Method 2's own spec requirement, never implemented) but MINOR in magnitude, mean residual only 0.09 m/s2, not the dominant effect. Strike-vector formula -- independently disproven as a hypothesis: `us=[1,pfx,0]/sqrt(1+pfx^2)` is used correctly by the analytic path too (produces tpv29's exact correct value), not a bug. `replaceSlaveWithMasterNode` -- independently disproven as a hypothesis: its dy-based check is geometrically correct for a fault at nominal y=0, the routine has no `C_elastic` dependency so it cannot explain a tpv29-vs-tpv30 difference, and this is consistent with the bit-identical mass measurement above. dx=200 geometry validation -- refused for BOTH tpv29 and tpv30 identically (2 of 202 boundary nodes over a one-sided truncation-error bound in `validateFaultRoughGeometry`), not tpv30-specific, a separate, minor, unexamined issue.

**The suspected defect, as it stood until 2026-09-21 (now CLOSED -- see the resolution paragraph after this one; the measurements here stay valid, the reading of them does not): an apparent fault-local equilibrium failure in the `C_elastic=0` path, present in BOTH plastic cases (`test.tpv30` AND `test.drv.a6`).** Class 2 (near-fault elements): tpv30 max residual 594 m/s2 / mean 5-13.5; drv.a6 max residual 395 m/s2 / mean 9.8. Class 1 (interior elements): tpv30 mean 3e-04 m/s2; drv.a6 mean 0.044 m/s2 -- 150x worse than tpv30's interior, ALSO unexplained. Symptom at `faultst000dp120`, dx=500: t=0 shear reads 33.051 MPa and relaxes -4.30 MPa over ~2s onto ~28.75 MPa. Target is 27.791 MPa (the prescribed `S.n`, which `test.tpv29` produces exactly and which the owner's own published cross-code data confirms is correct). The decisive evidence this is a real defect, not a discretization property: the owner's three published TPV30 submissions (100m/50m/25m) are RESOLUTION-INDEPENDENT (t=0 shear 28.176/27.888/27.815 MPa, only 1.3% spread over 4x mesh refinement) and show NO relaxation at ANY resolution (pre-arrival change +0.636/+0.640/+0.636 MPa, essentially identical) -- all converging toward 27.791. A discretization artifact would be a static offset that grows with element size; this is neither static (it relaxes) nor growing with coarseness (it's resolution-independent), so Method 2 (which `test.tpv30` correctly runs per spec) is proven to work correctly at every published resolution -- the defect is something else, specific to this build, not yet located despite seven measured-and-rejected hypotheses (see the exonerated list above).

**RESOLVED 2026-09-21: TPV30 has NO equilibrium defect.** mira-volkov's three-way control (dx=500, 4 ranks, `C_elastic=0`; HER measurement, labelled as such -- reproducible for the first time because the instrument that produced it, `EQDYNA_DUMP_EQUIL`, is now on master at `607f3c9`, item 52): residual acceleration with rough geometry mean 1.949e-01 / max 1.448e+01 m/s2; roughness zeroed 3.5e-14; rough geometry with the fault LOCKED 5.74e-14. The third row is the keystone -- same geometry, same pre-stress, same gravity, same split nodes, ONLY friction changed -- so the FE force balance on the rough fault is EXACT, and the 594 m/s2 class-2 residual above was the fault frictionally FAILING at t=0 on 304 of 3321 nodes (9.2%), with the spec's frictional cohesion (1.20 MPa surface / 0.40 MPa below 4 km, tapered) applied at `faulting.f90:162` -- those nodes fail WITH C0 included. **The owner question "is a 9.2%-at-strength initial condition intended?" is CLOSED**, on evidence the board editor re-derived fresh 2026-09-21 rather than inherited -- and one relayed description did NOT survive that re-derivation, corrected here rather than carried: "byte-identical on-fault stress construction, 21 lines, slots 7/8/49" is not confirmable (`grep -n 'fric\['` returns ZERO hits in either params file; the raw diff is 195 lines). What IS measured: stripping comments and blanks (96 vs 107 executable lines), `test.tpv29` and `test.tpv30` differ in EXACTLY two hunks -- `par.C_elastic = 1 -> 0` plus an 11-line plasticity/stress-parameter block, and `par.tpv = 29 -> 30`; friction, nucleation, stations and `par.dx` are identical. So the at-strength fraction cannot be a TPV30-specific construction error: TPV29 shares the construction, is gated, passes, and is validated against the owner's published SCEC results. **ONE INFERENCE, flagged as inference, not measurement:** the two cases ship differently decimated copies of the same official 25 m surface (50 m for TPV29, 100 m for TPV30); that both decimate to the same nodes at the shared `par.dx=500` is REASONED from the decimation scheme, never measured. **[2026-09-22: half settled, half refuted. The y POINT columns do land on the same official 25 m nodes. The DERIVATIVE columns did not: they were subsampled as point values out of two DIFFERENT source spacings, so tpv29 was fed 50 m slopes and tpv30 100 m slopes while both declared the dx=500 mesh -- an inference that was safe for the quantity it was made about and false for the one beside it. Fixed at `44afd4a`; `testsys/regression/test_rough_fault_normal_consistency.py::tpv30_copy_is_identical` now holds the tpv29/tpv30 copy relation mechanically instead of by reasoning.]** **What this closure does NOT touch:** the numpy/jax-vs-Fortran port divergence (item 19's row) and the rule 17 steps 4/7 registration decision both still stand -- `test.tpv30` stays unregistered. drv.a6's own class-2 residual (max 395 m/s2) PLAUSIBLY has the same explanation but that is UNMEASURED -- the dump instrument can now settle it and has not been run for that.

**LANDED 2026-09-22 -- the rough-fault surface-normal defect is fixed, both references moved, and the dx=100 evidence now reads against each case's OWN noise floor.** Order on master: `44afd4a` (merge), `e27e577` (gate evidence), `e1bba45` (perf ledger), `3388e40` (the dx=100 evidence). THE FIX: `case_input/test.tpv29/tpv29GeometryTools.py` subsampled the DERIVATIVE columns (`dydx`, `dydz`) as if they were point values, in both `convertOfficial25m` and `decimateToDx` -- a finite-difference derivative is a property of a grid AT A SPACING, so `y[::step]` is a valid coarsening and `dydx[::step]` is a category error. Derivatives are now `np.gradient(y, dx, edge_order=2)` recomputed at the mesh's own spacing. MERGE GATE, run by the conductor on the MERGED tree and not inherited from the branch: `python3 testsys/run.py all` exit 0, 2706.1 s; unit SUCCESS, regression SUCCESS, e2e SUCCESS 31/31 selected cells; `test.tpv29` max abs diff 0.000000e+00 (fortran) / 8.733900e-15 (python-numpy) / 7.981116e-14 (python-jax) against its 1.0e-10 bound; `test.drv.a6` SUCCESS on all three, untouched. Preserved at `docs/evidence/gate-44afd4a/`, raw log md5 `5b9ac9352605714506b6c4917418281e`. TWO REFERENCES MOVED, each its own rule-7 commit on the owner's recorded approval: `bbe6f1a` (`test.tpv29`) and `642f119` (`test.tpv30`); node coordinates identical in both. Re-derived independently by the board editor from the two blobs, not relayed: tpv29 median abs dfnft 0.1250 s over 2974 matched nodes, 9 arrival flips, nucleated 2974 -> 2983 of 3321; tpv30 median 0.3333 s, 16 flips to ruptured and 12 away, nucleated 2984 -> 2988. THE dx=100 RESULT THAT UNBLOCKED IT, re-derived by the board editor from raw canonical output and `scec_archive`, 80601 nodes per case, nearest-node match on (along-strike, down-dip): `test.tpv29` dx=100 (74966/80601 ruptured) vs archive 100 m median abs dt 0.0073 s / p90 0.0213 s and vs archive 50 m 0.0170 s / 0.0597 s; `test.tpv30` dx=100 (73630/80601 ruptured) vs archive 100 m 0.0107 s / 0.0397 s and vs archive 50 m 0.1087 s / 0.3487 s. **EACH CASE AGAINST ITS OWN NOISE FLOOR -- this is the correction, because the wrong version has been quoted repeatedly today:** the archive's own 100 m-vs-50 m self-sensitivity is median 0.0160 s / p90 0.0600 s for TPV29 and median **0.0920 s** / p90 0.3680 s for TPV30. The 0.0160 s figure is TPV29's and has been applied to BOTH cases; it is not TPV30's. The conclusion is unchanged and in fact stronger -- each case now agrees with the archive at or inside its own floor -- but no future reader may quote one case's sensitivity against the other. **SCOPE LIMIT, not to be overstated: `test.tpv30` is UNREGISTERED, so its regenerated reference is exercised by NO cell of the sweep.** What the gate covers for tpv30 is the GEOMETRY only -- `testsys/regression/test_rough_fault_normal_consistency.py`'s `shipped_surfaces_mesh_consistent` (3 shipped surfaces vs `np.gradient` of their own y at their own header dx, max abs diff 1.454e-14, bound 1.0e-04) and `tpv30_copy_is_identical`. Nobody may later read this gate as having validated tpv30's reference, and this landing does not touch the numpy/jax-vs-Fortran port divergence or the rule 17 steps 4/7 registration decision, both of which still stand.

**Two fix attempts were built, verified, and REJECTED -- do not resurrect either without new evidence:** (1) a discrete-equilibrium correction-offset (`T_init = analytic - FE reconstruction`, computed once at step 1, held constant) -- numerically verified to hit the target (27.7906-27.7915 MPa stable across the full pre-arrival window) but rejected: it cancels a symptom without finding its cause, and the resolution-independence evidence above means there IS a real cause to find. (2) A literal Method-1 architectural switch (drop gravity, use the element field for yield only, apply `T_init` directly) -- also rejected once the resolution-independence evidence came in, since it would abandon a method (Method 2) now proven correct, to dodge a defect that hasn't been located. Both implementation worktrees exist but neither lands; both approaches were tried and rejected, no further identification needed.

**The stakes -- `test.drv.a6` is GATED and running in CI today with a reference that encodes this exact unresolved behavior.** Its compset declares on-fault shear/normal directly (40.0 MPa / -120.0 MPa, `user_defined_params.py:134-135`), but its committed, currently-PASSING reference reads 24.68 MPa / 76.89 MPa instead -- the gate is internally self-consistent (numpy/jax match the Fortran reference) but there is NO independent published drv.a6 submission, so nothing has ever checked this case against external truth, and its flip-budget bound was calibrated AGAINST this exact behavior. Do not change drv.a6's reference or bound while this is open -- the gate's meaning is narrower than it looks (self-consistent, not externally validated) until this is resolved.

**Side findings from the investigation, not the main thread:** `test.tpv29` and `test.tpv30` run the SCEC spec's two mutually exclusive initial-condition methods (Part 8): Method 1 and Method 2 respectively. Nothing in either compset currently says so -- worth a comment in both `user_defined_params.py` files at some point (not landed). Pore pressure exists only implicitly, folded into `roumax - rhow` in two expressions, with `eleporep` hardcoded to `0.0d0` and the real formula commented out beside it in the source -- a fragile encoding; the owner's own recollection is that it was added to balance something and the reasoning was lost. Worth flagging as technical debt. `gamar` (over-pressurization ratio, default 0.66) is load-bearing in the stress construction and arrives purely by DEFAULT in `test.drv.a6`, which sets neither `gamar` nor `roumax` itself in its own compset -- an implicit dependency worth naming. `dtau` in `faulting.f90:9` is implicitly `SAVE`d and shared across ALL fault nodes -- a latent bug for `friclaw>=3` cases specifically (rate-and-state), not yet triggered by any currently gated case but worth a pathway row of its own if one doesn't already exist for it.

**Success criterion, written 2026-09-17 and SUPERSEDED IN PART by the 2026-09-21 resolution above:** `test.tpv30` at dx=500 must read 27.791 MPa shear / 181.504 MPa normal at `faultst000dp120`, matching tpv29 exactly at the same dx, with no pre-arrival stress relaxation -- the no-relaxation clause presumed the relaxation was a force-balance defect; with the relaxation identified as the fault frictionally failing at t=0 (intended initial condition, owner question closed), that clause no longer defines success, while the analytic target values themselves were never in dispute. Independent validation (rule 17 step 6) is already SATISFIED and does not need repeating: `testsys/parity/evidence_tpv30_scec_comparison.py` compares this repo's committed 500m Fortran reference against the owner's own published 100m 2015 SCEC submission and finds physics validity roughly matches (99.2% rupture-extent overlap, ruptured-area ratio 0.978-1.013, median final-slip diff 6.3%, Mw 7.026) -- that is a separate finding from the open port-vs-Fortran defect above and does not resolve it. Full numbers and trace: `NOTES_tpv30_gate.md`. This is the final summary of this investigation; do not append another correction on top of it -- edit this block in place only if new evidence arrives.

Two standing notes that are not items:

* **`writeCompTime` is read from nowhere** (`globalvar.f90:109`), so every
  per-stage timing number is invisible by default. That is how a 511x error in
  `compTimeInSeconds(2)` survived. Giving it an input slot is a case-input
  contract change; not queued, but it is why timing bugs hide here.
* **Four claims in this file did not survive measurement** (24(a)'s half
  tractions, a 33% JAX regression, a friclaw-4 coverage gap, a
  compile-fraction figure) and two nearly became fixes. Prefer measuring a
  claim over repairing it.

| 11 | ~~CHECK: `mesh4num` on-fault tolerance vs `checkIsOnFault` may diverge~~ RESOLVED BY INSPECTION (2026-09-16): the two passes CANNOT disagree, for two independent reasons. (1) STRUCTURAL: both call the same `checkIsOnFault` (`src/fortran/meshgen.f90:750`) with the same argument -- `countMeshEntities.f90:46` for the count, `createMasterNode` at `meshgen.f90:784` for the allocation -- and both pass the RAW GRID y. `insertFaultInterface` (`func_lib.f90:37-40`) writes the rough-fault y-blend to its `ycoort` output and never touches `nodeCoor(2)`; the blend reaches only `meshCoor(2,.)` (`meshgen.f90:72,805`). That separation is load-bearing, not incidental: `checkIsOnFault` tests `nodeCoor(2)==0.0d0` by EXACT float equality, which a blended y would break, so keeping the blend out of `nodeCoor` is what makes the exact comparison sound. Anyone tempted to 'simplify' by blending in place would reintroduce this item as a real bug. (2) ENFORCED ANYWAY: `meshGenError` (`meshgen.f90:403-409`, called at `:113`) compares `nftnd0` against `nftnd` per fault and calls `abortRun(ERR_MESH_FAULT_MISMATCH)` = exit 44 on any disagreement. So a divergence is a hard stop, not silent mesh corruption -- rule 2 satisfied without a new guard. NOTE: this row pointed at `mesh4num.f90` until 2026-09-16; that file is `countMeshEntities.f90`, and the earlier audit's claim that the allocating call is at `meshgen.f90:94,98` was also wrong -- those two are inside `if (C_degen > 3.0d0)` and tag wedge elements, not fault nodes. | 3 | `src/fortran/countMeshEntities.f90:46`, `src/fortran/meshgen.f90:750,784,403-409` | resolved 2026-09-16 | | `grep -n 'nftnd0(i)/=nftnd(i)' src/fortran/meshgen.f90` |

| 17 | DEFERRED BY DECISION (owner, relayed 2026-09-16 via the orchestrating session; not independently confirmed beyond that relay): the multi-fault support campaign is left for future work, not pursued now. **PREREQUISITE FOUND 2026-09-16, and it is not one of the four known bugs:** the INPUT CONTRACT is broken on the writer side. `scripts/case.setup` writes ONE on-fault station count on line 2 of `bStations.txt`, while `readInputFiles.f90:152` reads `(nonfs(i), i = 1, ntotft)` -- ntotft of them. They agree only at ntotft==1. Measured on a two-fault test.tpv8: the list-directed read runs off the short line into the station coordinates and the run dies with `Bad integer for item 2 in list input`, **exit 2**, a raw gfortran I/O error naming neither ntotft nor the cause. That crash also means the DELIBERATE multi-fault stop at `meshgen.f90:790` (`ERR_MESH_MULTIFAULT_MSNODE`, exit 45) is UNREACHABLE through the normal path -- the input read fails first, so a guard that exists has never once fired. Nobody has run ntotft>=2 through `case.setup` successfully, which is why items 7/9/10 have stayed latent: they sit BEHIND this. Writing ntotft copies of the one count would silence the crash and be wrong -- `nonfs(i)` is the count FOR FAULT i and `par.st_coor_on_fault` is a flat list with no per-fault assignment, so that assignment is the first real design decision of this campaign. `case.setup` now REFUSES ntotft>1 with a message naming ntotft, the file, the reader line and this item (rule 2), guarded by `testsys/regression/test_multifault_refused.py`, which also pins that ntotft==1 still works so the refusal cannot be satisfied by refusing everything. THEN the four known bugs: item 7 netcdf_io hardcoded fault index; item 9 output_onfault_st unit-51; item 10 MPI4arn stale-count allocation; item 11 (CLOSED 2026-09-16, cannot diverge). | 24 | (a) RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT (2026-09-16) -- **the half-traction claim does not reproduce.** Measured on test.drv.a6 (`C_elastic=0`, friclaw 4), 4,747 fault nodes deeper than 2 km, actual step-1 on-fault traction against `setPlasticStress`'s own formula (`strVert = -(roumax - rhow*(gamar+1))*depth*grav`, and at `str1ToFaultAngle=45` deg `cos(2t)=0` so Syy=strVert is the fault normal and Sxy=devStr the strike shear): **Tn ratio median 1.0018** (intended -1.3370e+08, actual -1.3377e+08), **Ts ratio median 0.9858**. The p10-p90 spread 0.93-1.08 is the fractal roughness perturbing the local fault normal, not a systematic factor. Probe: scratchpad/probe24b.py, reproducible from a serial drv.a6 case in ~2 min. MOST LIKELY 24(a) WAS item 26's `arn` doubling, fixed 2026-09-14: the mechanism matches exactly -- `totalMass = (massSlave+massMaster)*arn` in `faulting.f90:113-123`, so a doubled arn halves every traction, depth-independently. If so, item 26's claim that its changed path is 'provably never entered' by the gated compsets is WRONG for drv.a6, and that claim should be re-checked rather than trusted. **CORRECTED 2026-09-16 (owner-verified): that attribution is WRONG -- 24(a) was MIS-SCOPED, not explained by item 26.** `test.drv.a6` has a deliberately asymmetric y-domain (`ymin,ymax = -31/+30 km`, `ny=2`) putting the rank boundary at `y=-500 m` while the fault sits at `y=0` -- one grid cell off -- and it uses `insertFaultType=2` with `fymin=fymax=0`, i.e. the roughness is morphed into the physical y, so the case is topologically a PLANAR fault with no real nominal-grid y-extent, the precondition for entering `MPI4arn`'s add-back path at all. drv.a6 therefore never entered that path, either before or after item 26's fix, and item 26's arn-doubling bug could not have produced -- or explained the disappearance of -- 24(a)'s half-traction result. Item 26's 'provably never entered' claim in fact HOLDS for drv.a6 (for the structural reason above, not the domain asymmetry) but FAILS for tpv36/tpv37, which enter that path immediately (dipping faults, `C_degen>3` giving real nominal-grid y-extent) -- see item 26's row and `testsys/regression/test_dipping_fault_y_split.py`. Also worth recording: BEFORE item 26 landed, drv.a6 was protected from that exact bug ONLY by its 1 km y-domain asymmetry, not by anything structural -- a 'tidier' symmetric `-30/+30` domain would have put the rank boundary exactly on the fault and halved every traction, hitting the bug by luck rather than design. Do not tidy that domain. Consequence for item 32: the half-traction explanation for drv.a6's decomposition sensitivity is ELIMINATED -- the case runs with correct tractions, so the 329-flip serial-vs-4-rank floor is genuinely about decomposition. (b) RESOLVED 2026-09-17: Tv has a real input slot. `readInputFiles.f90`'s `tv = 2*dz/NUC_VS_FIXED` is gone; `tv` is read from `bGlobal.txt` (new viscoplastic block at the end of the file, written unconditionally by `case.setup`), and the Python port reads the same record instead of recomputing it. `par.viscoplasticRelaxTime = None` resolves to the old `2*dz/3464` in `lib.resolveViscoplasticParams`, so an unset case is bit-for-bit unchanged; TPV30 sets 0.05 s directly. A `bGlobal.txt` without the block is refused loudly on both sides (ERR_INPUT_FILE_STALE = 22), never defaulted. (c) RESOLVED 2026-09-17: `func_lib.f90`'s new `devStrDepthTaper` is SCEC TPV29/30's Omega(depth) and multiplies `setPlasticStress`'s `devStr`; depths come from `par.devStrTaperDepthStart/End` (both unset = no taper, exactly 1.0d0, so the multiply is an IEEE identity; a half-configured pair is refused). Ported to `func_lib.py`/`eqdyna3d.py` and gated by `testsys/regression/test_dev_str_depth_taper.py`, which compiles the REAL `devStrDepthTaper` and checks it against the spec's closed form AND against the port bitwise, at 12 depths, in both configurations (measured: max |fortran - spec| = 0.000e+00, fortran == port bitwise, Omega(19500 m) == 0.5 exactly). | 26 | ~~fault-plane/MPI-boundary 0.5x traction halving~~ FIXED (2026-09-14): root cause was `MPI4arn` (`src/meshgen.f90`) summing `arn` across an MPI-partition boundary in a fault-normal direction (zero nominal fault extent, per `fltxyz` -- always y, EQdyna faults are x-z-plane) as though it divided the fault surface, when it actually duplicated it (every rank touching that boundary already built the full local fault-node grid). Direct dumps (`scratch/mira_faultmpi_guard`): arn at an identical physical fault node = A serial/x-split/z-split, 2A under a y-splitting decomposition (1,2,1) -- and `fnms` (the OTHER quantity on the same `fltMPI`-gated exchange, `assembleGlobalMass.f90` `addFaultBoundaryTerm`) confirmed bit-identical across the same decompositions, i.e. NOT affected, no change needed there. Fix: `syncArnBoundary` now skips arn's cross-rank add-back (only) when `fltxyz(2,dimId,ift)==fltxyz(1,dimId,ift)` for that boundary's direction, while still setting `fltMPI(k)` and still exchanging data so `fnms`/`nodalMassArr`'s correction is untouched -- verified safe under a combined x+y split (2,2,1) too (no zero-mass/NaN; a prior attempt that skipped the whole `syncArnBoundary` call broke exactly that by silently skipping `fltMPI(k)`, which is why this fix is scoped to the arn add-back alone). `checkFaultMPIAlignment` (`src/eqdyna3d.f90`, called right after `meshgen`) is a permanent hard-stop guard for the one case the fix does not reason about (a fault with non-degenerate y-extent on a y-boundary -- not producible by any case in this codebase today); it no longer fires for the now-correct degenerate case. Guarded by `testsys/regression/test_fault_mpi_boundary_arn.py` (decomposition-invariance: serial==xsplit==zsplit==ysplit hypocenter traction on a tiny symmetric-y case; verified to FAIL if the fix is reverted). All 8 gated compsets use asymmetric y-domains (`fltnum(3)/(4)` are always 0 for them), so the changed code path is provably never entered there. CORRECTION (v5.5.0 release audit): this fix's source (`src/meshgen.f90` dimId threading, `src/eqdyna3d.f90` checkFaultMPIAlignment) was NOT actually committed when this line was first written three commits ago at 12c8274/e6ad307 -- only the regression test and this claim were; the diff sat uncommitted in the working tree, so `testsys/run.py regression` at those commits would have FAILED on a clean rebuild. The v5.5.0 release commit is what finally lands the source fix. e2e confirmation (v5.5.0 release gate, this session, src/eqdyna to avoid the reported concurrent `bin/eqdyna` TPV29 job): `python3 testsys/run.py all` -- unit/regression/e2e all SUCCESS, 8/8 e2e cases, 26/26 check.test.py comparisons SUCCESS, including test.tpv29 matching its frozen reference. | 30 | ~~BUG (instrumentation): `compTimeInSeconds(2)` is corrupted~~ FIXED (2026-09-16, 8839638) -- and it was **511x**, not the '<=1% bias, harmless' this row claimed. ONE global `startTimeStamp` (`globalvar.f90:201`) was written by EIGHT sites across six files, each pairing it with a different counter, so any nesting silently truncated the OUTER measurement. Real nesting: `eqdyna3d.f90:68-70` wraps `assembleGlobalMass`, which calls `MPI4NodalQuant` twice (`:41,:42`), each resetting the global -- so slot 2 timed only from the LAST such call. Measured before/after on test.tpv8 at 4 ranks: **compTimeInSeconds(2) 0.0001 s -> 0.0511 s**. Each site now uses a local `tStageStart`; `startTimeStamp` survives only in eqdyna3d.f90 where no nested writer remains. Output-neutral, verified: test.tpv8 x fortran max|diff| 3.051760e-11, identical to the pre-change sweep. **NEW, and the reason this went undetected:** `writeCompTime` (`globalvar.f90:109`) is initialised to 0 and read from NOWHERE -- not bGlobal.txt, not user_defined_params.py, not the environment. The per-stage timing output is unreachable without editing source and rebuilding. Giving it an input slot is a case-input contract change and is NOT done; until then every number in `compTimeInSeconds` is invisible by default, which is how a 511x error survived. | 2, 4 | `src/fortran/globalvar.f90:109,201`, `src/fortran/eqdyna3d.f90`, `driver.f90`, `assembleGlobalKU.f90`, `assembleGlobalMass.f90`, `calcHourglassResist.f90`, `faulting.f90` | fixed 2026-09-16 | `grep -c tStageStart src/fortran/*.f90` |

A finite-difference derivative is a property of a grid AT A SPACING: `y[::step]` is a valid coarsening, `dydx[::step]` is a category error -- it hands the solver 25 m-spacing slopes labelled as the coarse mesh's. Fixed on master today: derivatives are recomputed at the mesh's own spacing with `np.gradient(y, dx, edge_order=2)`. Landed `44afd4a` (merge), with `e27e577` (gate evidence), `e1bba45` (perf ledger) and `3388e40` (the dx=100 evidence) behind it; guarded by `testsys/regression/test_rough_fault_normal_consistency.py::shipped_surfaces_mesh_consistent` (3 shipped surfaces vs `np.gradient` of their own y at their own header dx, max abs diff 1.454e-14 against a 1.0e-04 bound). Evidence preserved at `docs/evidence/gate-44afd4a/`, raw log md5 `5b9ac9352605714506b6c4917418281e`. The dx=500 `bFault_Rough_Geometry.txt` md5 `00aea9b7...` quoted above was measured in v5.6.0, BEFORE this fix, and has not been re-derived since -- read it as history, not as a description of the geometry the gate builds today | 2, 3 | `case_input/test.tpv29/tpv29GeometryTools.py`, `scripts/lib.py`, `scripts/defaultParameters.py`, `testsys/regression/test_rough_fault_normal_consistency.py` | resolved 2026-09-15; corrected and re-resolved 2026-09-22 | `python3 -c "import sys;sys.path.insert(0,'scripts');sys.path.insert(0,'case_input/test.tpv29');import tpv29GeometryTools as g;print(g.faultGridForCase(50.0)[0].shape)"` |

| 33 | TO-DO (raised by the owner 2026-09-16): **re-measure JAX-CPU core scaling properly. Every scaling number this repo quotes is stale, unverified, or taken under load -- do not cite them until this lands.** WHAT IS ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED: one ratio, measured 2026-09-16 on the current code -- `test.tpv8`, **193 ms/step pinned to 1 core vs 13.8 ms/step unpinned = 14x**. That is all. WHAT IS NOT: the `2.00x / 3.96x / 7.48x on 2/4/8 cores` curve and the `~2.5x plateau when memory-bound` both PREDATE the Python restructure and the HLO-literal fix and have not been reproduced since. They were quoted in this session as if current; they are not. WHY THE OLD CURVE IS SUSPECT MECHANICALLY: this box is 8 NUMA nodes of 8 cores each (2 sockets x 32, AMD EPYC 7532, 1 thread/core, ~128 GB per node). The inherited knee sits at exactly 8 cores -- the NUMA node size -- so it may be measuring remote-memory cost rather than any property of the solver. One process realistically ceilings at one socket (32), and 64 was never a meaningful denominator for efficiency. THE EXPERIMENT: (1) `taskset` 1/2/4/8 cores WITHIN one NUMA node (cpus 0-7); (2) 8 cores deliberately SPREAD one-per-node (0,8,16,24,32,40,48,56) -- same core count, maximum NUMA distance, so core count and locality are separated; (3) 16 and 32 within one socket. Use a case with enough work per step -- **tpv104 or drv.a6, not tpv8** (1891 fault nodes runs out of parallel work early, which is a plausible alternative explanation for the knee). Report per-step cost by DIFFERENCE over two step counts, as `testsys/perf/run_perf.py` already does, so XLA compile is subtracted. Repeat each point and record `load_avg` per run. **REQUIRES AN IDLE BOX.** Every figure above was taken with 10 other users on the machine at load 6-11, plus this session's own gate. An idle box is a precondition, not a nicety -- a memory-bandwidth measurement under someone else's memory traffic measures them. **UPDATE 2026-09-17: two real tooling defects found+fixed in `testsys/perf/run_numa_scaling.py` (commit `03ba055`), not the measurement itself.** F1: the config generator produced an all-64-core configuration, contradicting this item's own "at most 32, not 64" constraint above -- removed. F2: configs jumped from spread-8 straight to socket-32 with no 16-core point, a 4x gap across exactly the region this item asks about ("16 AND 32 within one socket") -- added a generic two-NUMA-node ("multi-node-16" on this box's topology) point between them. Verified by `ast.parse` + `--help` only -- no idle box available this session to run the actual measurement; load-ceiling/override logic byte-for-byte untouched. The tool itself was wrong before today (would have misled even on an idle box) and is now fixed; the measurement is still outstanding, still blocked on load (ceiling unchanged at 1.0, load ~2.4-3.2 all session from other tenants). **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (commit `d70811d`): MEASURED, ROOT-CAUSED, PARTIALLY FIXED.** Owner's goal reframed mid-session to "make jax and numpy scale well" (the measurement above was always instrumental to that, not the deliverable); landed via mira-volkov, verified by wei-lin before landing. `testsys/perf/run_scaling.py` rewritten: `PY_THREADS` extended `[1,2,4,8]` -> `[1,2,4,8,16,32]` to match `FORTRAN_RANKS` (the tool previously could not say whether either python backend reaches 32 cores at all); bare `taskset` replaced with `numactl --cpunodebind/--membind` (binds CPU+memory together) on both the python path and the Fortran/MPI path (a new numactl-rankfile mechanism per rank, since a bare `mpirun --bind-to` can be reissued past an outer numactl restriction by OpenMPI's own hwloc binder); compact vs spread placement policies added so a knee can be attributed to locality vs out-of-parallel-work; reuses (does not duplicate) `run_numa_scaling.py`'s own busy-check. Measured (disclosed caveat: `--busy-ceiling 0.9` override, not the tool's strict 0.2 default, since ~9 other tenants held the box all session -- cross-validated two independent ways, exact ms/step numbers should be retaken on a quiet box as a follow-up, not treated as final): jax gains a real **2.35x at 32 cores** (cross-checked against this session's earlier clean 1-4-core numbers); numpy **anti-scales**, never faster than 1 core, mechanism verified by direct microbenchmark -- OpenBLAS reads its thread count at `import numpy` time only (confirmed by testing env-var-after-import vs numactl-pinned-before-start, not asserted). JAX's own HLO graph was dumped and read directly: one `fori_loop`, 1717 fusion + 556 scatter ops on arrays up to `f64[3818584]` per step -- real, large-scale structural parallelism exists, ruling out "nothing to parallelize" as the explanation for the plateau past 8 cores (memory bandwidth and/or the ~556 per-step scatter-accumulations are the more likely binding constraint, not chased further this pass). Both known project failure modes (a jitted function closing over an array paying an HLO-literal cost; `jax.jit` built inside a run function recompiling every call) were checked directly against `backend.py`/`eqdyna3d.py` and RULED OUT, not assumed clean. **Fix landed:** `_narrow_numpy_affinity()` in `src/python/eqdyna/eqdyna3d.py` (numpy backend only) pins the process to a single cpu, bounding the NUMA-migration worst case (a single-threaded process with wide affinity can be OS-migrated between NUMA nodes mid-run, stranding its memory -- measured ~2x regression from this specifically). This does NOT make numpy scale -- its hot kernels (`calcHourglassResist`/`assembleGlobalKU` elementwise ops, `np.add.at` scatter-adds, 85%+ of a step per item 40's profile) are single-threaded regardless of core count; a real fix needs a kernel rewrite, explicitly out of scope here. **A real regression caught by wei-lin's own review before it landed, not by the subagent's own testing:** the first version of the affinity fix picked `min(current)` -- the same lowest-numbered cpu for every process, verified directly (`/proc/<pid>/status` on two concurrently-launched processes both showed `Cpus_allowed_list: 0`) to collide every concurrent numpy cell in `testsys/e2e/run_e2e.py --jobs N>1` (this project's own wider local gate, `run.py all`) onto one physical core while dozens sit idle -- this exactly explained a ~10%-cpu-each pileup observed earlier in the session. Fixed to `ordered[os.getpid() % len(ordered)]` (still narrows to exactly one cpu, still fixes the NUMA-migration case, but different processes now land on different cpus -- re-verified: two concurrent processes now on cpus 25/27, not both 0). **Verification, independent of the subagent's report:** her own mission disclosed only 1 of 10 numpy cells (`tpv8`) re-checked against its frozen reference before she had to kill her own `run.py all` under this session's box contention; wei-lin ran the remaining 9 fresh on the corrected affinity code (`drv.a6, tpv10, tpv104, tpv1053d, meng2023a, meng2023cb, tpv29, tpv36, tpv37`, all python-numpy): 9/9 SUCCESS against their existing bounds, unmoved -- no reference moved, no bound loosened. jax/fortran cells are provably unreachable by this change (gated on `backend=='numpy'` in the diff itself), correctly not re-run. Diffed every changed file against current master before landing: zero staleness. Fresh `python3 testsys/run.py unit regression`: SUCCESS both tiers. **Still open, natural next step, not urgent:** re-measure the actual scaling curve on a genuinely idle box (this session never had one) for trustworthy ms/step numbers rather than the `--busy-ceiling 0.9`-override figures; jax's plateau past 8 cores is diagnosed as structural (real ops exist per the HLO dump) but not further isolated to memory-bandwidth vs scatter-accumulation specifically -- a controlled follow-up experiment, not queued. **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (this session, mira-volkov, worktree, commit `27c7a03`; NOT independently re-run number-by-number by the orchestrating session beyond spot-checking the evidence files exist and the diff was clean -- flagged as such):** re-measured with `testsys/perf/run_scaling.py --skip-fortran` at the tool's STRICT default busy-ceiling (0.2, no override). The box's busy-cpu set moved during the session (this row's own earlier entries already document ~9-tenant contention); this pass saw a DIFFERENT, also-moving set (cpu 0/1/10/16-19/30/40-41 intermittently over ceiling across 4 sweep attempts, per the landing commit message). Result: only 12 of 24 (PY_THREADS x backend x placement) configs survived the busy check; the other 12 were correctly SKIPPED and reported, not overridden -- `testsys/perf/scaling_last.json` (committed at `88f5227`) records exactly which configs were skipped, by label and cpu set; it does NOT carry a numeric per-cpu busy-fraction column, so read this paragraph, not the file, for the fraction/CPU claim above. Best clean JAX figure this pass: **2.18x at 8 cores (spread placement)**, which then REGRESSES to **1.41x at 16 cores (spread)** -- a declining trend, not a plateau approaching the standing 2.35x-at-32-cores claim. No 32-core point survived the busy check in any of the 4 attempts this pass, so the standing 2.35x figure is NEITHER confirmed NOR refuted by this measurement -- it remains unconfirmed on a clean box, and this pass's own trend (declining past 8 cores) is grounds for skepticism about it, not evidence against it. NumPy anti-scaling reproduces and is WORSE than previously characterized: not flat, it actively COLLAPSES from **1.40x (2 cores)** to **0.50x (4 cores**, i.e. slower than 1 core); 8/16/32 numpy configs were all skipped as busy this pass. STILL OPEN, stated plainly: a genuinely idle box (or a reserved/isolated allocation) is still needed for a trustworthy full 32-core comparison; this pass improves the honesty of the partial data (explicit per-config skip-vs-measured accounting) but does not close the measurement gap this row has been tracking. **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (this session, mira-volkov, worktree): TOOLING DEFECT F4 FOUND+FIXED -- placement was node0-first, not free-first.** Root cause of the prior pass's 12/24-skip rate: `compact_cpus`/`spread_cpus` always iterated `sorted(nodes)`, i.e. always started at node 0, regardless of where the box's free capacity actually was. This box's ~9 foreign tenants happen to sit on node0/node2/node6 intermittently, and 60 of 64 cores were free elsewhere the whole time -- the tool was asking for the wrong 32, not finding the box out of room. Fix: `free_node_map()` (new, in `run_scaling.py`) probes every cpu's busy fraction fresh before EACH configuration (reusing `run_numa_scaling.cpu_busy_fractions`, not reimplementing it) and restricts both policies to nodes ENTIRELY under the busy ceiling right now; `select_cpus()` returns `None` (recorded as a skip, exactly as a busy-cpu skip is) if the free set can't supply the requested core count. The ceiling itself (`--busy-ceiling 0.2`) is untouched -- this widens WHERE the tool looks for room, not what it demands of it. Node0-free case verified unchanged three ways: (a) synthetic unit check, `free_node_map` on an all-idle topology returns every node and `select_cpus` output is byte-identical to the old `compact_cpus(nodes, k)`/`spread_cpus(nodes, k)` for every k in 1..32 and both policies; (b) live run: when node0 was genuinely idle at measurement time, `th=1 compact`/`spread` picked cpu 0 exactly as before; (c) when node0 was NOT idle (observed live, cpu0 at 100% from an unrelated process), the tool correctly picked node1's cpu8 instead of skipping. **Re-measurement result: 23 of 24 configs measured** (only `python-numpy th=16 compact` skipped, cpu12 momentarily at 22%>20% ceiling) -- up from 12/24 the prior pass, on the SAME box, same strict 0.2 ceiling, no override. A genuine 32-core point was reached for the first time on all four combinations (numpy/jax x compact/spread) that reach 32, including one (`python-numpy th=32 compact`, `python-jax th=32 compact`) that substituted node4 for a momentarily-busy node2, live proof the free-first fix is load-bearing, not cosmetic. **Numbers** (`n_lo=10, n_hi=25` -- SMALLER than the tool's own 20/60 default; this box was measurably slower than nominal this session, see below, and 20/60 was cut short mid-sweep to fit the session, disclosed here rather than silently used): jax COMPACT is the clean, monotonic curve this item has been chasing -- 1.00x(1) / 1.50x(2) / 1.72x(4) / 1.91x(8) / **2.52x(16)** / 1.98x(32) -- peaking at 16 cores, mild falloff at 32, net ~2x there; this CONTRADICTS the prior pass's own "REGRESSING to 1.41x at 16" finding, which was drawn from a 12/24-skip pass that never reached a clean 16 either -- 16 was reachable this time and is the best point, not a regression point. jax SPREAD is noisy at 16/32 (16: nonsensical -160 ms/step negative slope, i.e. t_hi < t_lo -- a per-step-by-difference artifact from the shortened step window plus real run-to-run variance; 32: 0.82x, below 1x) and should NOT be read as a locality verdict either way; a wider step window on a quieter box is needed before drawing a compact-vs-spread conclusion at 16/32. NumPy stays flat at ~1.70-1.72 s/step across ALL 1..32 cores and BOTH policies except two isolated slow points (spread n=2: 2.76 s/step; spread n=16: 2.55 s/step) that look like transient contention hits, not a structural collapse -- this does NOT reproduce the prior pass's "COLLAPSES from 1.40x(2) to 0.50x(4)" claim, which was measured on the OLD node0-first tool under a different (also transient) contention pattern; the two passes are not measuring the same thing and neither supersedes the other pending a clean run at the full 20/60 step count. **Caveat carried forward, same as every prior pass on this box:** this was NOT an idle box (load 4.5-7.2 all session, ~9 foreign tenants, node2 at 1.6 GB free of 129 GB throughout) -- the busy-ceiling check makes every individual number honestly-measured on genuinely-idle cpus, but does not make the BOX idle, and does not rule out cross-node memory-bandwidth contention that a per-cpu ceiling cannot see (a `test.tpv104` 20-step run that should take ~40s per the committed reference took 170s+ under transient node2/node6 pressure earlier in this same session, on cpus the ceiling had certified as 0% busy -- flagged, not chased further here). `testsys/run.py unit regression`: SUCCESS both tiers, run through the correct interpreter (the venv at `/home/utig5/dliu/gns/gns/venv_cotopaxi/bin/python3`, which is what `python3` resolves to on this box's normal PATH and is the only one with `jax` installed -- worth naming explicitly since an earlier invocation this session under a hand-built PATH resolved to the system `/usr/bin/python3` instead, silently losing jax and false-failing `test_stress_i0_carry_aliasing.py`; corrected before any numbers were trusted). **UPDATE 2026-09-18:** dispatched to build a controlled diagnostic distinguishing "(a) scatter-add specifically fails to CPU-thread-parallelize" from "(b) memory bandwidth is the shared limiter" for the 2.52x@16c / 1.98x@32c jax-compact plateau -- landed (commit `036b4ba`): `testsys/perf/probe_scatter_bandwidth.py` (report-only, not wired into any tier, touches no solver kernel/`matrix.py`/`testNameList.py`), `NOTES_item33_scatter_probe.md`, `testsys/perf/scatter_bandwidth_last.json` (committed at `036b4ba`). The tool isolates a matched-total-bytes elementwise control op (no scatter) against a jax duplicate-index scatter-add op (N_SOURCE=3818584 f64, the real per-step HLO array shape for test.tpv8; N_TARGET=477323 assuming ~8 hex elements share each interior node -- a stated, not measured, assumption), both jitted, compact-pinned at 8 vs 32 cores via `run_scaling.py`'s own free-node-aware numactl pinning (reused verbatim, no second implementation), timed by n_lo/n_hi difference (30/100 fori_loop iterations) to remove compile cost. Subagent's original claim: verdict (a) -- scatter regresses (0.75x-0.85x speedup 8->32c across 2-3 runs) while control "never regresses" (1.03x-1.76x), refined by an HLO finding that both ops get identical XLA partition counts (3 @8c, 6 @32c), so the mechanism isn't simple partition starvation -- inferred (not measured) as likely inter-socket write contention on the shared scatter destination buffer. **wei-lin's re-verification before landing, the operative finding, not the subagent's original claim:** re-ran the identical committed script 3 more independent times, same sha, same interpreter. One run gave control speedup = 0.74x -- a real regression that directly falsifies "control never regresses." Applying the script's OWN verdict threshold (verdict (a) requires control_speedup>=1.5 AND scatter_speedup < 0.6*control_speedup) to all 5 independently completed runs (2 original + 3 wei-lin's): only the very first run actually meets that bar. The other 4 read as "(c) inconclusive" by the script's own identical logic, and one of the 3 re-verification runs (control=0.99x, scatter=1.02x) INVERTS the claimed asymmetry (scatter edges out control). The box's busy-check printed a live multi-tenant roster each run (cisco-amp-scan-svc, cosmo, cshyu, enze, junlin, lp, markw, messagebus, rdomeyko, ron, yukoo, zjia) -- a genuinely shared login node, not the "finally quiet" box the dispatch assumed. **Corrected standing verdict: NOT SETTLED.** The HLO partition-count finding (3@8c/6@32c, identical for both ops) is a compile-time property unaffected by runtime noise and still stands as-is. The timing-based scatter-vs-control asymmetry is at most a weak, directionally-suggestive signal (scatter's speedup sits at/below control's in 3 of 4 valid paired runs; aggregate means ~0.91x scatter vs ~1.14x control) -- the box's per-run noise floor is comparable to or larger than the effect claimed, so it cannot be reported as a mechanism finding. This does NOT close item 33's standing "needs an idle box" gap -- it sharpens it: even the box available this session was materially busier than assumed, and the probe as built does single-shot timing per config with no repeated-trial averaging, which the next attempt should add alongside the still-unrun `perf c2c`/coherence-counter follow-up (named in `NOTES_item33_scatter_probe.md`'s own open-questions section) to actually confirm or kill the write-contention hypothesis.
