But it assigns `outer_dimension_partitions` to the per-step scatter-add fusion SUB-LINEARLY with core count, confirmed by dumping real HLO (`XLA_FLAGS=--xla_dump_to=...`) and reading `backend_config` at 1/4/8/16 pinned cores: 1->none (fully serial), 4->"2", 8->"3", 16->"4" -- 16x more pinned cores buys only 4x more partitions, a compile-time decision baked into the shipped `libjax_common.so`, not exposed by any XLA_FLAGS. Every candidate knob was tried and measured, not assumed: `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, `TF_NUM_INTRAOP_THREADS`, `TF_NUM_INTEROP_THREADS` -- all had ZERO effect on thread count or partition count. Setting `XLA_FLAGS` to ANY value at all (even a single well-formed flag like `--xla_cpu_multi_thread_eigen=true`) made things WORSE: thread count rose from 42 to 64-65 at 8 cores and wall-clock roughly TRIPLED (11s -> 26-49s for 5 steps) -- this empirically justifies why `run_scaling.py` has never set XLA_FLAGS (previously an assumption, now a measured fact). The historical pre-restructure code that tried to set `intra_op_parallelism_threads={n}` via XLA_FLAGS was independently confirmed NEVER TO HAVE WORKED (malformed flag, no `--` prefix, silently ignored by absl -- and even if well-formed, XLA_FLAGS itself is harmful as just shown). This is now the SETTLED verdict for this specific question, consistent with and explained by the HLO finding above (both ops get the same kind of under-partitioned HLO from the same cost model) -- mark the scatter-vs-bandwidth question CLOSED as (b), superseding the "NOT SETTLED" language, and note this plainly as a real overturn of the prior session's leaning finding, not a confirmation of it. **jax vs Fortran ms/step, matched cores, `test.tpv104`, compact placement (n_lo=20, n_hi=60, repeats=2, busy-ceiling 0.2 default, NOT overridden):** cores 1/2/4/8/16 -> Fortran ms/step 947.70/491.75/269.57/161.69/119.15 (speedup 1.00x/1.93x/3.52x/5.86x/7.95x); jax ms/step 612.19/411.20/333.85/249.12/149.54 (speedup 1.00x/1.49x/1.83x/2.46x/**4.09x**). Changed to per-cpu filtering. (2) `fortran_cmd` was missing `--bind-to none` on the `mpirun` invocation -- without it, OpenMPI's own default core-binding (active whenever np>1) pre-restricts each rank's cpu affinity mask before the script's per-rank `numactl` call runs, and numactl then fails with "cpu argument N is out of range" trying to move the rank elsewhere. **Every Fortran cell with np>1 was failing outright before this fix; only np=1 happened to pass, masking the bug.** Independently reproduced by wei-lin before landing (not taken on the subagent's report alone): the exact numactl/mpirun command fails with "cpu argument 32 is out of range" (exit 1) without `--bind-to none`, and runs clean through to the expected refusal (exit 21) with it, on 4 ranks across 2 NUMA nodes. | 34 | OPEN (2026-09-16): **grid-scale stress ringing at 500 m is stabilised but not damped.** After the rupture front passes, on-fault shear rings at ~1.4-3.2 Hz with 0.7-5.6 MPa peak-to-peak. `Vs/(2*dx) = 3.46 Hz` at dx=500 m, so this is the mesh's shortest resolvable wavelength. MEASURED, post-arrival window [t_arr+0.15, t_arr+1.6] s, linear detrend only: tpv36 (wedge) 0.68 MPa / 3.17 Hz / 1.67 pct of peak tau; tpv37 (wedge) 0.76 / 2.58 / 1.77; **tpv10 (hex, dipping) 5.60 / 2.37 / 9.49**; **tpv104 (hex, vertical) 4.79 / 1.41 / 5.86**; tpv8 (hex, vertical) **0.004 / 1.39 / 0.01** -- essentially ring-free. WHY IT IS NOT SUPPRESSED, and this part IS settled: the default hourglass control is `C_hg=1`, KF78, STIFFNESS-based and non-dissipative by construction. Its only damping is the Rayleigh term inside the hourglass force, `dl = disp + rdampk*vel` with `rdampk = 0.1*dt` (`readInputFiles.f90:133`), giving zeta = 0.5*(0.1*dt)*omega_grid = **0.0065, i.e. 0.65 pct of critical**, an e-folding after ~24 cycles. The mode cannot grow but barely decays. AND ZETA IS RESOLUTION-INDEPENDENT: `rdampk ~ dt` while `omega_grid ~ 1/dx ~ 1/dt`, so they cancel -- 0.65 pct at both 500 m and 50 m. Refining does NOT damp this mode; it moves it to a band carrying less energy, which is why the published 50 m run looks flat rather than being better damped. THE LEAD: `C_hg=2` is a genuine VISCOUS hourglass control (`kapa_hg=0.1`, Goudreau and Hallquist 1982) that IS dissipative, and **no case in this repo sets it** -- that path has never been exercised. One run of tpv37 with `C_hg=2` would say whether it damps this at the default kapa_hg. WHAT IS NOT KNOWN: why the amplitude varies 1000x across cases (tpv8 0.01 vs tpv10 9.49). Element type is NOT the discriminator -- the degenerate-wedge cases ring LESS than two of three hex cases, and a degenerate wedge is still an 8-node element of the same shape family, so the hex hourglass modes apply normally. friclaw, dip, insertion-vs-degeneration and nucleation sharpness are all confounded at n=3-9 stations per case. Three mechanisms were proposed and measured WRONG before this (front element-crossing, wedge hourglass mismatch twice); do not add a fourth without a controlled experiment. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE GATE: the 500 m reference files CONTAIN this ringing, so the sweep compares ringing against ringing and is structurally blind to it. Acceptable for a regression check; not an accuracy claim. **UPDATE 2026-09-17 (dunyu-liu, model sonnet, this session -- relayed, NOT independently re-run by the orchestrating session; treat as a hypothesis carried from her report until independently reproduced, same disclosure pattern as item 40):** C_hg=2 (the viscous hourglass control this row's "lead" section names) was measured on `test.tpv37` and does NOT work as a drop-in fix at its only implemented coefficient (`kapa_hg=0.1`, hardcoded in `src/fortran/calcHourglassResist.f90`). Finding: C_hg=2 reduces on-fault ringing amplitude by roughly 2x where rupture survives, but it does so by materially over-damping the rupture itself, not by selectively suppressing the zero-energy hourglass mode -- 3 of 12 sampled on-fault stations arrest entirely (peak slip rate drops to roundoff, ~1e-15 m/s, zero final slip) and stations that still rupture lose 30-67% of final slip. At the one station comparable in both runs and far enough from the forced-nucleation patch to be a clean comparison (`faultst000dp180`), ringing amplitude and final slip drop by the SAME proportion (~57%), which is the signature of bulk damping, not frequency-selective suppression. Also newly established: `C_hg` is NOT exposed as a settable case parameter anywhere in this codebase -- it is compiled in at `src/fortran/globalvar.f90:105` (`C_hg = 1`), with zero references in `readInputFiles.f90`, `scripts/defaultParameters.py`, or `scripts/case.setup`. The measurement required a temporary source-level edit (`globalvar.f90:105`, hardcoded to 2), run in a worktree, then reverted -- confirmed reverted, `git diff master -- src/fortran/globalvar.f90` is empty, nothing lands from this experiment. Baseline reproduction at C_hg=1 (station `faultst080dp120`, sanity check before trusting the C_hg=2 number): 0.741 MPa / 2.44 Hz / 2.61% of peak tau, vs this row's recorded 0.76 MPa / 2.58 Hz / 1.77%. Amplitude and frequency reproduce within 2.5%/5%; the percent-of-peak-tau figure is ~47% relatively higher than recorded and unexplained (likely a different arrival-threshold or peak-tau normalization convention in whatever produced the original number, not chased further) -- flagged as a methodology gap in reproducing this row's own historical baseline, not as a new physics finding. Natural next step, not done, not queued: whether a smaller `kapa_hg` (e.g. 0.01-0.03) could damp the mesh-scale mode without arresting rupture is untested. Only tpv37 (a wedge case) was tried; the hex cases with much larger recorded ringing (tpv10 5.60 MPa, tpv104 4.79 MPa) were not tested and may respond differently. Command to reproduce (informal, no committed script yet -- this was an exploratory worktree experiment, not landed tooling): temporarily set `C_hg = 2` at `src/fortran/globalvar.f90:105`, rebuild, run `test.tpv37` via the normal `create.newcase`/`case.setup`/`mpirun -np 4 eqdyna` path in a scratch dir (not the committed compset), compare on-fault station time series against the C_hg=1 baseline in the window `[t_arr+0.15, t_arr+1.6]` s. | 2, 4 | `src/fortran/calcHourglassResist.f90`, `src/fortran/globalvar.f90` (C_hg, kapa_hg, rdampk), `src/fortran/readInputFiles.f90:133` | until characterised | 2026-09-16 | one run of tpv37 with par.C_hg=2 vs the default |

Given its own numbered row so it is findable by grep rather than buried only in the v5.8.2 Tasks-done paragraph (~line 172) and `docs/SESSION_LOG_2026-09-16_v5.8.2-followon.md`: whether to rewrite git history to purge the ~1.16 GB of `scec_archive/` blobs that `f2c9851` accidentally tracked (the `.gitignore` inline-comment bug, fixed in v5.8.2; the tracked files were `git rm --cached`'d, but the blobs stay reachable in history through the tags below). FRESH NUMBERS (verified 2026-09-16, that session, commands in the Command column): repo is public, 2 forks, 24 stargazers, `diskUsage` 290272 KB (~290 MB); `git count-objects -vH` reports size-pack 283.57 MiB across 4392 objects in one pack; traffic over the last 14 days is 976 clones, 158 unique cloners. TRADEOFF (background, still accurate, kept for the record): a history rewrite (`git filter-repo`/BFG) would diverge the history of all 158 recent unique cloners and both forks -- neither is fixable from this side -- and would move the SHAs of the `v5.8.0`/`v5.8.1`/`v5.8.2` tags (the 3 of this repo's 20 tags cut after `f2c9851`) and their GitHub Releases. It would also NOT shrink GitHub's stored copy by itself: unreachable objects from a rewrite stay in GitHub's existing pack file until GitHub Support runs gc server-side, so the disruption is paid up front and the bytes are not reclaimed by the rewrite alone. Against that: 290 MB is roughly 29% of the 1 GB size GitHub starts commenting about -- not yet an urgent size problem on its own. This tradeoff background is why the decision landed where it did, not a recommendation being re-litigated. | 4, 12 | `.git/` (repo history), `docs/SESSION_LOG_2026-09-16_v5.8.2-followon.md`, `docs/SESSION_LOG_2026-09-17_v5.9.0-followon.md:48` | decided 2026-09-17 | `git count-objects -vH; git stash list` |

## Historical SCEC submissions

`scec_archive/` (gitignored, at repo root) holds EQdyna's own SCEC/USGS code-
verification submissions in raw upload format, one directory per
benchmark/version/resolution — e.g. `scec_archive/tpv29/eqdyna-v3.1-100m-2015/`
(the published `dliu` entry; its cplot verified byte-identical to the copy the
cvws public area serves). Treated as read-only historical reference for
comparing new runs against past published standing. Not tracked by git and not
reproducible from the repo — back it up independently.

## Release roadmap

Work is grouped into release campaigns; each ships only when its own gate is
green (fast tiers + e2e + CI) and its claims are test-backed.

| Release | Theme | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| v5.5.0 (cutting now) | TPV29 revival + MPI fix | TPV29 gated (dx=500, reference frozen, FULL_SPECS at 50 m); fault-on-MPI-boundary arn bug fixed + hard-stop guard + regression test; insertFaultType=3 documented; memory/cell report corrected; compset figures |
| v5.6.0 | Fault-geometry robustness | Validator for bFault_Rough_Geometry.txt (header vs case grid, dx/fxmin/fzmin, row count, finite values, derivatives vs finite differences with a self-consistent curvature bound, per-cell offset limit, provenance-sidecar cross-check), called from case.setup for EVERY insertFaultType>0; Fortran-side checks in read_fault_rough_geometry (was reading nnx*nnz rows unverified) with a deterministic non-zero stop; `scripts/convertFaultGeometry` for arbitrary supplied surfaces; provenance sidecars on every generated file; case.setup made idempotent and non-clobbering via `par.faultGeometryWriter` (compsets no longer write geometry as an import side effect -- that is what silently replaced a deliberately placed 50 m TPV29 surface); unit tests per failure mode plus a Fortran regression guard; items 27 and 31 |
| v5.7.0 | Viscoplastic path + TPV30 | item 24 (Tv input slot, setPlasticStress depth taper, the +7.3215 constant, lib.py rho, plastic-output window), item 23 (Drucker-Prager kernel unit test), then TPV30 promoted and gated (draft built, reproduces the elastic-vs-plastic contrast) |
| v5.8.0 | Cycle + archaeology | item 16b (mode-2 restart gate coverage - the EQsimu hand-off path has none today), item 19 (TPV2802_50_a6 gating; TPV2800/2801 dead-branch decision) |
| v5.9.0 | Benchmark suite build-out (owner-queued, after the viscoplastic path) | Compsets + gated references for the remaining benchmarks EQdyna has submissions for: **TPV30** (rough + viscoplastic, draft already built and validated against TPV29's contrast), **TPV34** (Imperial Fault in the real CVM-H 3D velocity structure - needs the 3D-velocity input path exercised), **TPV35** (Parkfield 2004 M6 - SCEC's first VALIDATION benchmark, compares against recorded ground motion, not just cross-code), **TPV36/TPV37** (15-deg shallow-dipping thrust; submissions are modern-code v5.3.3 at 50 m, so their published standing is a current-code datapoint and the best available cross-check). Each: fast-tier compset at a gate-sized dx with frozen reference, FULL_SPECS entry at the spec resolution, compset README with the published standing from scec_archive/, and scoring against the public cvws metrics where a metric page exists |
| v5.10.0 | Fully dynamic cycle support (EQsimu) | Gate the mode=2 restart path using `liu2020.fdc.planar` (single fault, planar, dx=300, RSF aging, term 150 s - the Liu et al. 2020 EQsimu configuration) as the canonical cycle compset. Two layers: (a) EQdyna-only gate from a FROZEN EQquasi hand-off committed as test data (fault.dyna.r.nc + restart netCDF) - fast, exercises netcdf_read_on_fault_eqdyna_restart and the mode=2 path that has zero coverage today; (b) a periodic coupled verification running the real loop across EQdyna / ~/seas_bp10_eqquasi / ~/eqsimu, not in CI. Also covers liu2020.fdc.rough.250 and bp1001.fdc.rough.250 once (a) works.
**Plus build `liu2020.fdc.kink.300`** — the missing EQdyna half of the Liu et al. 2020 paper's kink-bend fault: EQquasi already has `liu2020.qdc.kink.300` and `.600` (an active revival there), so the pair completes the coupled configuration. It is also the only route to gated coverage of `C_degen` wedge degeneration, which no current gated case exercises despite v5.3.3 re-incorporating it for TPV36/37. Interface contract (frt.txt format, restart nc variables, fric slot layout) written down as part of this release |
| v6.0.0 | Multi-fault, then multi-fault cycles | Two stages. (1) Multi-fault dynamic rupture: fix items 17 and 7/9/10/11, and adopt EQquasi's ALREADY-DEFINED fault-major layout `on_fault_vars(ntotft, nfz, nfx, 100)` in EQdyna's netcdf writer and `netcdf_read_on_fault_eqdyna` (EQdyna is `(nfz, nfx, 100)`, single-fault; the hardcoded fault index 1 of item 7 lives in exactly that reader) rather than inventing a second convention - reference compsets `bp1002.qdc.2500`, `test.stepover.qdc.{con.}1000` in ~/seas_bp10_eqquasi/compset. (2) Multi-fault FULLY DYNAMIC cycles: build the EQdyna-side `bp1002.fdc.*` counterpart (EQquasi has only qdc step-over today) and extend the v5.10.0 mode=2 hand-off to carry multiple faults. Cross-repo campaign (EQdyna / ~/seas_bp10_eqquasi / ~/eqsimu) with a written interface contract and matched tags - major bump, coordinated release |

Not on the roadmap (external, idle-window, or owner-scheduled): strong-scaling
measurement (`testsys/run.py scaling`, needs an idle machine; a cells-per-rank
study is in flight), the TPV29 50 m spec run (48 ranks, ~5 h, running) and its
scoring against the public cross-code matrix, spec-resolution verification runs
on HPC, TPV38 (awaiting SCEC's TSurf release).

## Goals

- **Python EQdyna (vectorized)** — long-term goal set 2026-09-12: a vectorized
  Python implementation of EQdyna, developed under `python/` at repo root
  (ALL Python solver development lives there). Phase 1: feasibility spike —
  port the hot kernel + time loop for `test.tpv8`, parity-gated against the
  Fortran `frt.txt` golden gate, with wall-clock comparison (NumPy first,
  JAX/GPU assessment after). Fortran `src/` remains the production/HPC engine;
  the Python solver targets test-scale cross-verification first, GPU later.

PRE-TAG AUDIT (victor-reyes + lars-eriksson) found and this release fixed: (1) CRITICAL -- `.gitignore`'s `scec_archive/` pattern carried a trailing inline comment, which gitignore does not support as a comment, making the whole line an unmatchable literal; `scec_archive/` was therefore NOT ignored and f2c9851 tracked 693 files / ~1.16 GB of fetched SCEC data into git despite every doc saying it stays untracked. Pattern fixed, files `git rm --cached`'d this release; the blobs remain in history pending a deliberate owner-approved rewrite (NOT done here). (2) the commit message's "3477 fault nodes" for the arn audit does not match the actual evidence (`3416`, recorded twice in code comments and in the regression guard's own printed output) -- 3477 was a transcription slip; 3416 is the reproducible number and is what ships in the code comments and this note. (3) stale case-param comments in test.tpv36/37 still described the guard as refusing rather than noticing; corrected. (4) the Python C_degen refusal message wrongly claimed tpv36/37 were "declared UNSUPPORTED in matrix.py" (they are not; correct answer is they are not gated at all); corrected. (5) `testsys/parity/evidence_tpv36_37_vs_published.py` hardcoded an ephemeral agent-session `/tmp` path (rule 4); now refuses without `EQDYNA_EVIDENCE_SCRATCH` set, consistent with the pre-existing spike-only-evidence pattern in `testsys/parity/`. (6) README's exit-code table regenerated after a hand-edit attempt broke `test_stop_exit_status.py`'s drift check -- fixed at the source (`errorCodes.f90` comment) and regenerated via `--update`, not hand-edited again. PROJECT_RULES.md's own index was one rule short (rule 17 existed in body, not in the index) -- added. DEFERRED, not fixed (needs owner judgment): purging the 1.16 GB from git history; the stale `v5.5.0 (cutting now)` / theme-vs-actual-content drift in pathway_forward.md's Release roadmap table (pre-existing, predates this release). Gate: unit 214 SUCCESS, regression SUCCESS (18 guards, after fixing the doc-drift regression this release introduced and re-running clean), e2e (`run.py all`) 24/24 in 1123.1s, e2e-ci 10/10 SUCCESS | v5.8.2 |
